## When a Shopify Store Needs a Blog
Use when: Deciding whether to invest in a blog and how much to prioritize it for an ecommerce store.
- Identify search terms with informational intent that shopping pages can't rank for — e.g., 'best cold press juicer' returns informational results, so a listicle/blog review is required.
- Publish the blog post (and related posts on juicer types and usage) alongside the shopping pages so you capture that traffic.
- Recognize the main benefit per the source: blog posts support the rankings of shopping/collection pages through internal linking and topical authority — the source claims ~80% of SEO revenue typically flows through collection pages.
- Convert blog traffic into revenue by embedding direct product CTAs, capturing emails via a discount popup, and running retargeting ads to blog visitors who didn't purchase.
Caveat: The 80% revenue-through-collection-pages figure is the presenter's heuristic, not a universal calculation.
Caveat: Informational content takes time to rank; treat it as support for commercial pages.

Tags: search, blog-content, topical-authority, internal-linking, collection-pages, shopify, retention
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Optimizing Blog Post Titles, H1, URL, and Meta Description
Use when: Creating or auditing a Shopify blog post for on-page SEO.
- Title tag: put the target keyword at the start, stay within ~60 characters (the source's guidance), and add long-tail keywords or modifiers where possible.
- H1: keep it simple — essentially the target keyword, e.g., 'types of women's underwear'.
- URL: keep it short and use the target keyword, mirroring the H1.
- In Shopify, scroll to the bottom of the blog post editor and click 'Edit website SEO' to set the page title, meta description, and URL handle.
- Include the target keyword in the meta description — it doesn't materially help rankings per the source, but the keyword appears bolded on the SERP, improving click-through rate.
Caveat: Character limits are the presenter's stated guidance; SERP truncation varies.
Caveat: Meta descriptions affect CTR, not rankings per the source.

Tags: search, blog-content, on-page-seo, shopify, ctr
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Writing Blog Content That Beats What Ranks
Use when: Drafting a blog post meant to rank for a specific informational keyword.
- Source post ideas from your topical map (built during keyword research) rather than random keyword lists.
- Inspect the pages currently ranking for the term; note their structure and headings.
- Make your post cover at least the same topics and subtopics, ideally more, so it provides comprehensive coverage of related questions your audience asks.
- Judge depth, not just breadth — topical authority considers how thorough each post is, so avoid thin content.
- Break up text with a mix of images, videos, infographics, tables, and charts; the source cites Quirky Quinn's infographic-heavy 'types of women's underwear' post as an example.
- Use these visuals to influence engagement metrics (bounce rate, time on page) that indirectly help SEO.
Caveat: No specific content-length or visitor-duration benchmarks are established.
Caveat: Visual engagement effects on SEO are described as indirect, not guaranteed.

Tags: search, blog-content, content-strategy, topical-authority, ux
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Embedding Product CTAs and Internal Links in Blog Posts
Use when: Monetizing blog traffic and using blog posts to strengthen product/collection pages.
- Embed relevant direct product CTAs within posts or at the end, chosen to match the post topic (e.g., a hip-and-joint-supplements post ends with a CTA to that product).
- Build internal links from blog posts to shopping pages and to other relevant blog posts, creating a thematic network that helps search engines understand the site and distributes page authority.
- Instead of building all backlinks to shopping pages (which looks unnatural), build backlinks to good blog posts and internally link those posts to shopping pages to distribute link equity more naturally.
- Implement the internal links deliberately — the source notes many agencies publish blog content without appropriate internal links, wasting the SEO value.
Caveat: Internal linking to shopping pages distributes authority but doesn't replace direct backlinks entirely.
Caveat: Keep links relevant; link-stuffing dilutes relevance signals.

Tags: search, blog-content, internal-linking, link-building, conversion-optimization, shopify
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Building Topical Authority One Cluster at a Time
Use when: Planning blog/content production across topics for an ecommerce store.
- Pick one topic cluster (e.g., cold press juicers) and publish all content around that topic: how to make cold press juice, whether it's healthy, why it's better, etc.
- Understand that one collection page alone won't build authority — comprehensive coverage of the topic is what establishes topical relevance.
- Once authority is established in a cluster, expand gradually to related topics (e.g., masticating juicers) by creating a connection post such as 'cold press vs masticating juicers' first, then building out that next cluster.
- Use the approach to differentiate from large retailers (Amazon, Target, eBay) that can't produce these supporting blog posts and therefore can't claim industry-level topical authority.
Caveat: The 'one cluster at a time' sequencing is the source's recommended method, not a proven universal rule.
Caveat: Timelines vary; no specific ranking timeline is guaranteed.

Tags: search, blog-content, topical-authority, content-strategy, keyword-research
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## What Ecommerce Link Building Is and Why It Matters
Use when: Planning off-page SEO for a Shopify or ecommerce brand.
- Treat backlinks as votes from other sites signaling that yours is valuable and trustworthy to search engines.
- Check the backlink profiles of the top-ranking sites for your competitive keywords — the source says you'll typically see hundreds or thousands of backlinks.
- Build a deliberate backlink strategy rather than relying on on-page SEO alone.
- Understand the five link types covered as relevant to ecommerce brands: foundational, guest posting/niche edits, and others to be detailed in the full link-building training.
Caveat: The presenter is Chris Graham, co-founder of 607 Media, in charge of off-page SEO — treat his claims as his professional view.
Caveat: Backlink counts cited are the source's general observation, not a fixed benchmark for any niche.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, off-page-seo, acquisition, competitive-strategy
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Foundational Links for a New Ecommerce Site
Use when: Onboarding a new ecommerce site with no backlink profile.
- Claim basic links such as business directories and social media profiles.
- Treat these as easy-to-acquire foundation signals rather than ranking drivers — they tell search engines your site exists and is not fake.
- Use them to send early relevance signals about what your site is about.
- Layer guest posting and niche edits on top of foundational links once they're in place.
Caveat: Foundational links alone won't rank competitive terms; they're a starting base, not a strategy.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, off-page-seo, foundational-links, ecommerce
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

## Map the five link-building types to an ecommerce backlink mix
Use when: Planning a quarterly off-page SEO plan for a store and deciding where to spend outreach time and budget.
- Treat foundational links (social profiles, business/citation directories, Web 2.0 sites) as the base layer that signals your site exists and what it is about; the source says these are mostly free and Google-endorsed, so build them first before anything else.
- Make guest posts and niche edits the 'bread and butter' for ecommerce: guest posts let you control content, anchor text and the target URL, so you can point a link from an interior-design blog to a specific curtain collection page. Niche edits add your link to an already-published, already-trafficked article, which the source argues is faster and often more powerful because existing pages pass link equity immediately.
- Use expert-quote platforms (the source calls it HARO, now rebranded Connectively — verify the current name/interface) to answer journalist queries in your category, e.g. advice on choosing curtains for bright rooms, and earn editorial links when quoted.
- Build linkable assets (comprehensive guides, infographics, calculators) on your own site, then do light outreach to relevant sites telling them the asset exists and asking for a link.
- Use digital PR — a data-driven insight or press release pitched to journalists and bloggers — when you want the highest-quality editorial links.
- Decide per link whether to target the domain or a collection/product page: the source recommends 50–75% of links to the main domain in the first ~3 months, with the rest on low-hanging-fruit collection pages.
Caveat: The 50–75% domain-share rule is the source's rule of thumb for the first three months, to be adjusted afterward based on competition and results.
Caveat: Tool names (Semrush, Ahrefs, HARO/Connectively, Snov.io, Hunter) change interfaces and pricing — verify current functionality.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, off-page-seo, backlinks, guest-posting, digital-pr, ecommerce-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Judge a backlink's quality before pursuing it
Use when: Vetting a prospect site or an inbound link offer before you write a guest post, accept a placement or pay a fee.
- Check dofollow vs nofollow: dofollow links pass value; nofollow passes little. The source says a natural mix is fine, but focus effort on dofollow links.
- Review anchor text: the visible clickable text should be descriptive and relevant, e.g. 'office chairs' for an office-chair page, so search engines receive the right topical signal. Keep it natural — over-optimized anchor patterns stand out against competitors.
- Assess page-level relevance and site-level relevance: the article should relate to the page it links to, and the host site should sit in or near your industry (an interior-design site is a good fit for a curtain brand).
- Check authority metrics (Moz Domain Authority, Ahrefs Domain Rating): aim higher, but treat them as manipulable — read them alongside the other factors rather than as the sole test.
- Consider the linked page: send links to the page you actually want to rank, such as a collection page, but don't wildly exceed competitor link counts — if competitors have ~10 links to a page, building 30 'sticks out like a sore thumb' and risks penalties.
- Prefer a few high-quality links over many low-quality ones.
Caveat: Authority metrics can be gamed; the source explicitly says to interpret them in context, not in isolation.
Caveat: The source presents over-building relative to competitors as a penalty risk; this is the presenter's framing, not a stated Google threshold.

Tags: search, link-building, backlinks, link-quality, anchor-text, off-page-seo, ecommerce-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Run a link-gap analysis and set a monthly link target
Use when: Setting link-building targets for a store and deciding how many placements per month to buy or earn.
- In Semrush or Ahrefs, filter your backlinks to dofollow only and to referring domains with DR/DA of at least 30 (the source's chosen floor). Count the result — e.g. 105 referring domains.
- Apply the identical filters to your SEO competitors' profiles; the source analyzed three or more and averaged around 203 referring domains in its example.
- Subtract your count from the competitor average (203 − 105 = ~98 referring domains needed) to size the gap.
- Convert the gap into a cadence: spreading 98 links over 24 months is about 4 new links per month, but the source recommends aiming for roughly 6–8 per month to keep pace as competitors also build.
- Re-run the link-gap analysis periodically to confirm you are still competitive.
Caveat: The 105/203/98 figures are an illustrative example, not a benchmark for any particular niche.
Caveat: The DR/DA 30 filter is a source-specific heuristic; adjust the floor upward if your own profile is stronger.

Tags: search, link-building, link-gap-analysis, measurement, semrush, ahrefs, off-page-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Screen spam links with a five-point checklist
Use when: Evaluating a paid link offer, an inbound guest-post pitch or an email offering backlinks for a store.
- Check it is a real site people care about: open it, judge design and content quality, and see whether it writes about one industry or about everything — general 'we cover anything' blogs are often link-selling sites to avoid.
- Check history with the Wayback Machine: if the domain previously covered a different topic (the source's example: a former casino site now presenting as interior design), treat that as a red flag.
- Check niche relevance: the site should discuss what your brand is about. Avoid general blogs built solely to sell links.
- Check organic traffic: look for real traffic that is trending upward or at least stable. The source's bare minimum is 1,000 visitors per month, and sudden traffic dips can signal a penalty.
- Check the site's own backlink quality: if the host site's inbound links are poor, it passes poor link equity to you.
- Avoid bought links from cheap marketplaces (the source cites Fiverr and unsolicited backlink emails) — low quality at best, ranking damage at worst.
Caveat: The 1,000-visitors-per-month minimum is the source's stated bare minimum, not an industry standard.
Caveat: The source notes you can double-check cheap links because some may be fine, but says they usually are not.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-quality, spam-links, backlinks, prospecting, off-page-seo, due-diligence
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Build foundational links and citations as the base layer
Use when: Launching a new store or a new brand with no backlink profile, before starting guest posting or outreach.
- Build foundational links — social profiles, business directory and local citations, plus Web 2.0 pages such as Medium or Behance. The source frames this as 'entity stacking': helping Google understand who you are so it can rank you.
- Do not just create the profile and drop your URL in the about page: fill in the full business information and post some content and images so the listing sends strong identity signals.
- For citations, prepare a standard data set: business name, owner name, address, city, telephone, website URL, business description, target keywords (e.g. shopping-page keywords) and logo. The source says you likely already have this and AI can help draft descriptions.
- Target at least 30 foundational links — the source's own internal target, described as safe and free, though fewer may suffice.
- Consider outsourcing citation building, as the source does, because doing it manually takes a lot of time.
Caveat: The 30-link target is the source's internal goal, not a calculated requirement.
Caveat: Vanity or incomplete profiles add little; the value is in filled-out, active listings.

Tags: search, link-building, foundational-links, citations, entity-seo, new-store, local-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Prioritize low-hanging-fruit collection pages for links
Use when: Choosing which collection or category pages to build links to this month on a store.
- Open Semrush's Organic Research tool for your domain and filter positions to 4–10.
- The output is the set of your pages sitting on page one but not yet in the top three — the source's 'low-hanging fruit' that can be pushed into the top three fastest.
- Pick roughly three to five of these pages per month depending on your link-building budget, and save them as your monthly link targets alongside the main domain.
- Balance this against the source's 50–75% domain-share rule for the first three months: juice the root domain while also pushing the easiest collection pages.
- Rebalance toward collection pages after a few months based on competition and observed results.
Caveat: The instruction is written for Semrush but the source says it is translatable to Ahrefs.
Caveat: The 3–5 pages and 50–75% domain split are rules of thumb, explicitly dependent on brand age and existing link profile.

Tags: search, link-building, low-hanging-fruit, collection-pages, semrush, prioritization, ecommerce-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Prospect link targets by scraping competitors' guest-post backlinks
Use when: Building an outreach prospect list for a store's guest posting and niche edit campaign.
- Determine SEO competition — the brands winning the SERPs for your target keywords, which may differ from your brand competitors. Use Semrush's Keyword Overview to read the SERP for a key term such as 'sheer curtains'.
- Pick mid-sized niche competitors rather than giants: the source excluded Target, Walmart and Ikea because scraping their backlinks yields mostly unreachable or irrelevant prospects. Collect at least five SEO competitor domains.
- In Semrush Backlink Analytics, open the Backlinks tab for a competitor and filter to: dofollow links, one link per referring domain, 'content' placement (so the link sits inside article content, implying a guest post), and platform = blog. Export the results.
- Run the exported domains through Semrush's Bulk Analysis tool set to root domain, then filter to sites with monthly visits greater than 1,000 and an authority score of 15 or above (raise the threshold if your own profile is stronger).
- Repeat for the other SEO competitors to build a refined prospect list.
- If the list is thin, expand prospecting beyond SEO competitors to any site in your niche, or in adjacent niches (for curtain brands: interior design, home improvement, family/mom blogs).
Caveat: The 1,000 visits and authority-score 15 thresholds are the source's heuristics.
Caveat: Stay natural: the source warns against clearly irrelevant placements, e.g. an outdoor blog linking to a curtain brand.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, prospecting, link-building, guest-posting, competitor-analysis, semrush, outreach
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Find contact details for link prospects
Use when: Turning a filtered prospect list into a sendable outreach list for guest posts or niche edits.
- Use a paid email-finding tool as the primary route — the source recommends Snov.io based on its results and clean lists, and notes Hunter.io as another option used by some SEOs; verify current pricing and data coverage.
- If not paying for tools, go site by site into the contact page and look for a named person's email (chris@domain.com) rather than a generic inbox (info@domain.com), because a real person is less likely to bin the message.
- Check the about page for owner names, then find their social profiles and reach out there.
- Use site contact forms: the source says these almost always reach the site owner, and a well-crafted message can get a reply.
- Send direct messages on social platforms referencing their site and asking whether they accept guest posts.
Caveat: Manual research takes far more time and may not yield a usable contact, which is why the source prefers paid tools.
Caveat: Tool names and capabilities change; confirm the current product before subscribing.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, prospecting, outreach, email-finding, snov-io, hunter-io, guest-posting
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Write outreach subject lines and bodies that get opened and answered
Use when: Drafting the first outreach email to a blog or site owner to request a guest post placement.
- Make the subject line both highly relevant and attention-grabbing — the source says if it only grabs attention without relevance, it reads as spam. Include the recipient's name and their domain, e.g. 'Quick question, Chris, about curtains.com'.
- Use curiosity: a question about their own site works because it piques the owner's interest.
- Use humor where it fits your niche — the source says jokes in the subject plus a matching answer in the body have worked well for them, and suggests testing combinations of tactics in your own niche.
- In the body, state your intention to write content for their site and the type of content you specialize in, and make clear a dofollow link is desired.
- Say explicitly that you are willing to pay any publishing fee if required — the source says you will most likely pay for these links.
- Keep it short and to the point, and customize the free template rather than sending it verbatim: the source argues generic templates underperform because everyone has the same one.
Caveat: The source states Google does not officially support buying links and may treat manipulative links as link spam, while asserting that paid links are widely used and work when done properly — treat this as the source's position, not a safe harbor.
Caveat: The claim that a well-crafted message beats ~70% of template users is the source's assertion, not a measured benchmark.

Tags: search, outreach, email-copywriting, guest-posting, link-building, subject-lines, paid-links
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Warm Up Cold Outreach Sending Accounts Before Guest Post Pitching
Use when: Preparing the email sending infrastructure for a link-building or blogger-outreach campaign on behalf of an ecommerce store.
- Choose an outreach sending tool. The source recommends Instantly as its top pick; Hunter (for finding prospect emails) also has an outreach feature, plus Lemlist and GMass are named options. Verify current pricing and features, as tools change.
- Warm up each sending account first: send a small volume of emails that are designed to be opened, read, and replied to (not cold pitches), gradually increasing that volume until the account builds a healthy sending reputation.
- After warm-up, begin with roughly 20 cold emails per day from each account and increase little by little over a few weeks.
- Limit how many email accounts you create under one domain — the source says too many 'at-yourdomain.com' accounts starts to look suspicious. If you need high volume, buy additional related domains and set up accounts under those.
- Complete the technical setup (SPF/DKIM-type configuration) the platform guides you through before sending.
Caveat: Tool names, free trials, and interfaces change — verify on the provider site.
Caveat: The 20-emails-per-day starting figure is the source's heuristic, not a universal rule.

Tags: search, link-building, outreach, cold-email, email-deliverability, guest-posts, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Make Guest Post Pitches Unique Instead of Reusing a Template
Use when: Writing the outreach email to a blog or site you want to earn a guest post or link from.
- Start from a short, direct template: introduce yourself by name and domain, and state your request plainly.
- Before sending, research the specific site and its audience, then clearly explain what the value of your proposed post would be for that audience.
- Personalize the message so it reads as crafted for that site rather than mass-sent.
- Keep it to a quick question format that is easy for the recipient to reply to.
- Expect to outperform the roughly 70% of senders who rely on a generic template — the source's claim, based on the reasoning that a widely-shared free template loses effectiveness as more people use it.
Caveat: The 70% figure is the presenter's assertion, not a measured benchmark.
Caveat: Template personalization improves reply odds but does not guarantee acceptance.

Tags: search, link-building, outreach, guest-posts, email-copywriting, prospecting
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Close and Negotiate the Guest Post Link, Then Run Final Vetting
Use when: Replying to a positive outreach response and deciding whether and at what price to place a guest post or link on that site.
- Treat any reply asking for clarification as a positive signal, not just an outright yes.
- Run final vetting on the site: check language/locale, security, organic traffic, and other quality metrics using SEMrush or your tool of choice. Much of this may already have been filtered earlier, but double-check before committing.
- Once the site passes vetting, negotiate terms — if you are buying the link, discuss price. Use SEMrush Authority Score-based pricing benchmarks as rough guidance, but treat them as flexible: a highly relevant site with strong traffic may reasonably cost more.
- Remember the goal throughout: secure the link, whether free or paid. Each step should move you closer to that.
Caveat: Pricing benchmarks are the source's rough guidance and can shift with inflation and time.
Caveat: A positive reply is a question or interest, not yet a placed link — keep negotiating.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, guest-posts, negotiation, vetting, outreach, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Write a Guest Post That Fits the Host Site's Audience
Use when: Writing a guest post for a partner blog (e.g., an interior design site) that will link back to one of your store's pages, such as a product collection.
- Identify the host site's actual audience and write for them. Example: for an interior design blog, write for interior designers and design enthusiasts, not for your curtain shoppers.
- Pick a topic that is directly relevant to the page you want to link to. For a sheer curtains collection page on a curtain store, the guest post should be about sheer curtains.
- Define your target page first, then generate title ideas — you can use AI (e.g., ChatGPT) to brainstorm titles and descriptions that combine the target keyword with the host blog's theme, then refine the output through iteration.
- Choose a title that contains the target keyword and also fits the host blog's topic (the source's example: a title including 'sheer curtains' for an interior design blog).
- Write 600–1,200 words unless the host specifies otherwise; note that some hosts who want longer, high-quality content are willing to publish without a fee, so a free link may be worth extra length.
- Keep grammar, structure, and coherence human-readable; the source's reasoning is that content readable by humans is readable by search engines.
- Offer fresh insight and a friendly, approachable tone — a good guest post can also position your brand as a thought leader in its niche.
Caveat: Content-length guidance varies (the source gives both 500–1,000 and 600–1,200); treat it as directional, and defer to the host's requirements.
Caveat: AI output needs refinement — the source notes you often have to 'wrestle' with it.

Tags: search, link-building, guest-posts, content-writing, keyword-targeting, ai-writing, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Place Guest Post Anchors Naturally and Near the Top
Use when: Adding the link back to your store's collection or product page while writing a guest post.
- Write the anchor into a relevant sentence within the body content, close to the top of the post.
- Aim for seamless integration where the link enhances rather than interrupts the reading experience.
- Avoid isolating the link in a standalone spot (e.g., 'check out sheer curtains here'). The source notes some sites require this format for link inserts, but it is not ideal.
- Incorporate the target keyword near the beginning of the title where practical, and use keywords in subheadings only where they fit naturally — forcing them in is risky.
- Prioritize natural placement over hitting every keyword-stuffing opportunity.
Caveat: Some hosts mandate a fixed anchor placement (often for link inserts); when they do, accept the constrained format.
Caveat: Over-optimizing headings with keywords can read as unnatural and is risky.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, guest-posts, anchor-text, on-page-seo, content-writing, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Diversify Anchor Text and Mirror Competitor Backlink Profiles
Use when: Deciding what anchor text to use when placing guest posts or link inserts to a specific store page.
- Understand the anchor types: exact (identical to the target keyword, e.g., 'sheer curtains'), partial (includes keyword words or adds to them, e.g., 'sheer curtains like these'), URL (the literal page URL), brand (your store name), topical (related terms like 'home decor curtains'), and miscellaneous ('click here' or the linked page's title).
- Use mostly partials, URLs, and miscellaneous anchors in the early months of link building — this is the safest default.
- Reserve exact-match anchors for very good, high-value sites, and even then use only one or a few. Using the same exact anchor across every backlink can look unnatural to search engines.
- Use synonyms and near-variations of your keyword to add partial-match diversity.
- To determine the ideal mix, analyze the backlink profiles of your top three competitors — match the anchor-type ratio that dominates your space so you don't stand out as an outlier.
Caveat: The source says an unnatural ratio is a risk to avoid, not a guaranteed penalty — 'better safe than sorry.'
Caveat: Rigid ratios can be relaxed once your profile is more established (the source suggests around months six to seven you can start optimizing further).
Caveat: Exact-match ratios are the source's opinion, not a search-engine-stated rule.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, anchor-text, backlink-profile, competitive-analysis, off-page-seo, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Run Final Checks After a Guest Post Goes Live
Use when: After a host publishes your guest post, to confirm the link is correct and valuable.
- Check that the link resolves to the intended store page; paid or sponsored links must be qualified with sponsored or nofollow rather than requiring do-follow.
- Confirm the link is contextual — it sits in a good, relevant sentence rather than sticking out awkwardly.
- Review the published post to ensure it was not edited in a way you disagree with; if changes were made, decide whether you are okay with them.
- Then repeat the whole process: craft pitches, send them, write posts, build links — the source emphasizes this is a repeatable loop that compounds your backlink profile over time.
Caveat: Hosts may edit your post; you may need to accept or negotiate changes after publication.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, guest-posts, link-verification, off-page-seo, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Evaluate relevant link inserts without assuming faster rankings
Use when: Choosing between building a new guest post or paying to insert a link on an existing relevant page on a partner site.
- Look for a site that already has existing articles relevant to your content.
- Prefer pages that already have traffic — a relevant, already-ranking page is a much stronger link target.
- Prepare your response: research the topic, ensure your answer is knowledgeable and credible, and format it for easy readability.
- Check the domain rating or authority score of the source site with your tool before deciding whether to pay — skip very small sites unless they are highly relevant. There are free web tools that return domain rating, and SEMrush gives an authority score.
- If the page is highly relevant, pitch the insert even if the site is anonymous or lower-authority, because relevance still makes it a good backlink.
- Once you have found a relevant existing page, ask about a link insert rather than a guest post; expect most hosts to request a fee.
- Ask about link inserts during the negotiation process on any outreach, in case they are available.
Caveat: There is usually a fee for link inserts, though they yield an aged, already-indexed link that can act faster than a fresh guest post.
Caveat: The source warns not to overdo link inserts — balance them with guest posts.
Caveat: Editorial qualification: paid placements must use rel="sponsored" or rel="nofollow"; do not buy unqualified links to manipulate rankings. Assess sponsored placements by relevant referral traffic and commercial value, not promised ranking credit.

Tags: search, link-building, link-inserts, niche-edits, off-page-seo, backlink-strategy, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

## Pitch ecommerce expertise to journalist sourcing requests
Use when: Looking to earn high-authority, low-cost backlinks for a store by being quoted as an expert in your niche.
- Sign up at Connectively (the platform that replaced HARO), verify your email, and choose the 'subject matter expert' role to get started.
- Fill out your profile thoroughly, since journalists may read it. Describe your expertise in your core niche (e.g., curtains) and related topics (e.g., interior design, home improvement) so you match more queries.
- Start on the free/light plan so you can test whether the strategy suits your niche before committing.
- Prospect for queries: sign in, use the topic filters aggressively to narrow the flood of queries, and browse each relevant question.
- For each query, check the sender site's authority (domain rating via a free tool, or SEMrush authority score). Answer relevant queries even from lower-authority sites, and pitch even anonymous queries if they are relevant — journalists don't always disclose their outlet.
- Track your pitches in a spreadsheet (see the dedicated tracking card) so you know whether the strategy is working before scaling it.
Caveat: Connectively is HARO's newer home — the platform name and any pricing/free plan may change, so verify.
Caveat: The strategy works better for some niches than others; if you can't find relevant queries, other link-building methods may be a better fit.
Caveat: Platform names in the transcript may be obsolete; verify an active journalist sourcing service before following signup instructions.

Tags: search, link-building, haro, connectively, pr, expert-positioning, backlinks, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

## Pitch ecommerce expertise to journalist sourcing requests
Use when: Writing an answer to a journalist's source query on Connectively/HARO for your ecommerce niche.
- Research the topic so your response is knowledgeable and credible; even if you are the expert, some research is fine.
- Start with a Google search for a general overview, but do not stop there — reporters can Google too, and they want expert insight they cannot get from a generic search.
- Explore Quora and YouTube for additional expert perspective (vet the channels), and draw on personal or first-hand experiences, which cannot be copied.
- Dive into Reddit to gauge real user perspectives and shape your answer accordingly.
- Focus on the human touch: relate your answer to a specific experience or event, especially with AI-generated content becoming common.
- Format for skimmability: bold the key words and phrases and use bullet points so the journalist can grasp your answer instantly.
- Keep a short bio (one paragraph is best, two max) saved in a notes file or doc, ready to paste into answers.
- Submit carefully: once submitted, pictures can no longer be edited in Connectively — you can only view them on your dashboard.
Caveat: Journalists receive many responses — readability and genuine expertise raise your odds but do not guarantee selection.
Caveat: The bio length and formatting tips are the source's guidance, not platform rules.
Caveat: Platform names in the transcript may be obsolete; verify an active journalist sourcing service before following signup instructions.

Tags: search, link-building, haro, connectively, content-formatting, expert-positioning, backlinks, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx

---

## Pitch ecommerce expertise to journalist sourcing requests
Use when: Running an ongoing HARO/Connectively effort and deciding whether to invest more time or team members.
- Set up a spreadsheet with columns that let you log each query, the site/outlet, its authority, whether you pitched, and the outcome.
- Keep your submitted pitches in a separate file or folder, and link to each one from your tracking sheet so you can retrieve them.
- Update the sheet every time you hunt and every time you respond to queries.
- Track your funnel numbers: for example, out of 20 queries answered, maybe 2 get positive replies, and out of those 2, 1 closes into a link. If that rate is consistent, scale the effort — assign more people or have one person answer as many queries as they can.
- Stay alert for journalist responses: Connectively notifies you by email when a pitch is accepted. Set a daily reminder to check that inbox.
- If a journalist asks for more info, they will do so by email — respond as soon as you can, before the deadline. Their requesting longer, more detailed answers and pictures is a good sign.
- Only make a scaling decision from your tracking data, not from a single outcome.
Caveat: The conversion example (2 replies and 1 link from 20 queries) is an illustrative example, not a promised rate — your numbers will differ by niche.
Caveat: Journalist acceptance is the gate; positive-reply behavior patterns are the source's observation.
Caveat: Platform names in the transcript may be obsolete; verify an active journalist sourcing service before following signup instructions.

Tags: search, link-building, haro, connectively, tracking, outreach-measurement, backlinks, shopify-seo
Source: Beginners Guide to Shopify SEO - Full Course (4+ Hour Masterclass).docx
