## Read an Ahrefs-Style Domain Rating and Backlink Profile
Use when: Benchmarking a store against competitors before deciding whether link building, content or on-page work deserves the next sprint.
- Read the dashboard in the source's order: DR (out of 100, driven by backlink profile), UR for the specific URL, total backlinks, and unique referring domains.
- Understand the ratio: 17.7K backlinks from 2.5K referring domains means many domains produce multiple links - the source treats unique referring domains as the more meaningful count.
- Combine with organic keywords and estimated organic traffic, and ignore paid keyword counts unless you are also running ads.
- Filter organic keywords by position to isolate top-10 rankings, and check the intent labels (informational, commercial, transactional, branded) to see whether the rankings sit on pages that can sell.
- Note new tools in the source also report AI visibility metrics (e.g. presence in AI overviews) - treat these as emerging, separately-tracked indicators, not substitutes for organic data.
Caveat: The source shows that different tools produce different traffic estimates for the same site (Ahrefs ~4-5K vs Semrush ~9.8K) and different authority scores (44 vs 35) - never compare scores across tools.
Caveat: DR/UR are third-party relative metrics, not Google positioning signals.

Tags: search, ahrefs, backlinks, measurement, competitive-analysis, link-building
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

## Compare Ranking Position Over Time to Find Lost and Declined Keywords
Use when: Quarterly keyword triage: identifying product or category searches the store used to rank for and has slipped away from.
- Open the Organic Keywords report for the target country and click the Position column to select top-10 (or top-3) results, then show results to see first-page rankings.
- Move the date comparison to the previous month or previous 3 months; the source's example showed a keyword slipping from position 3 to 6.
- Apply the lost/improved/declined filters and focus on declined and lost entries - the source states explicitly that these deserve the main effort.
- Cross-check period-level totals: the source's example showed a peak around 14,000 keywords in March 2024 versus roughly 3,390 later, with many lost terms visible in the prior-period view.
- For each lost or declined keyword, decide between content modification, new supporting content or link building, informed by what competitors now use that you do not.
Caveat: Not every lost keyword is worth recovering - the source pairs this with the requirement that keywords must match real services the store provides.
Caveat: Keyword counts and position history are tool estimates, not Google's internal data.

Tags: search, ahrefs, keyword-research, content-strategy, competitive-analysis, measurement
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

## Attribute Traffic Loss to Specific Pages Using Peak-Period Comparison
Use when: When total organic traffic is falling and you need to separate pages that lost rankings from pages that merely lost clicks.
- In the traffic report, view six months to a year to locate the peak and the start of the decline (the source's example: October 10,344, November 8,692 and still falling).
- Open the Top Pages section and set the comparison date to your best month.
- Read the loss column per page: the source's example showed one page falling from 2,261 visits in March to 174 by November.
- Flag pages showing traffic in the peak period but zero now, and check whether they still load. The source found a page that opened fine but received no traffic, indicating it is no longer ranking ('N/A' position) rather than broken.
- Translate findings into a fix list: lost keywords and their source pages define the content and link priorities.
Caveat: A page loading normally with zero traffic points to loss of ranking, not a technical outage - diagnose accordingly.
Caveat: Peak-month selection is judgemental; traffic seasonality in retail can make a peak month a poor baseline.

Tags: search, ahrefs, measurement, site-audit, content-strategy, product-page
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

---

## Judge Backlink Quality with Referring Domains, DR and Spam Score
Use when: Reviewing a store's inbound link profile after outreach or a link-building campaign, and cleaning up toxic links.
- Sort the backlink report by DR ascending and descending: the source notes zero-DR referring domains contribute nothing, while high-DR links (the example cited DR 78) carry more weight.
- For each suspicious domain, check its spam score with a free spam-score checker; the source's threshold is under 5 being fine, above roughly 10 risky, and 10-30 requiring removal or disavowal.
- Note the follow/nofollow distinction: the source treats dofollow as the link type that passes ranking value and nofollow as delivering no SEO benefit.
- Inspect the anchor-placement style: an example showed a third-party article linking a brand name to an About page, which the source uses as a legitimate quality example.
- Compile spam links into a notepad list of domains and upload it to the Google Search Console disavow tool (which requires Search Console to be installed and uses the same Google login).
Caveat: The numeric spam-score bands are the source's heuristic, not a Google-published standard.
Caveat: The source also notes that a relevant but low-authority or new site can be left in place - relevance and age can offset a weak score.

Tags: search, ahrefs, backlinks, link-building, disavow, google-search-console
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

---

## Run an Ahrefs Site Audit and Prioritise Errors by Colour
Use when: Setting up a recurring technical audit for a store, particularly before or after a replatform or URL restructure.
- From the dashboard use Create Project, either importing from Search Console or adding the domain manually (a manual project continues without analytics verification and updates weekly).
- Add target keywords and competitor domains if you have them, then run the crawl and open the health score (the source's example scored 96 - excellent - yet still had errors worth fixing).
- Treat red errors as priority: 404 pages (redirect to the nearest relevant page or homepage), canonical pointing to redirect (copy the correct canonical URL into the tag so it matches the page rather than a redirected variant), orphan pages with no internal links (link them from a menu, blog or article), https pages containing http internal links, and oversized image files.
- Treat the source's explicit rule for canonical mismatches: first check whether the link works; if not, the redirect explains the gap; if it works, align the canonical tag with the page's own URL.
- Hand the exported error list to a developer, since most fixes require back-end access.
Caveat: A high health score does not mean the site is clean - the source's example flagged errors at a 96 score.
Caveat: The source notes developers can resolve most red errors but the auditor cannot change them directly without back-end skills.

Tags: search, ahrefs, site-audit, technical-seo, redirects, canonicalisation
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

---

## Write Meta Titles and Descriptions Inside the CMS Within Length Limits
Use when: Clearing yellow audit warnings on product, collection and blog pages, and improving click-through from search results.
- From the audit warning list, open the URL whose title or description is flagged, and use a browser SEO extension or source view to read the current character counts (the source's example: a 73-character title and a 167-character description).
- Determine the page type first (collection, product, blog) because the editing location differs in each CMS.
- For a Shopify-style store: admin > Products > Collections to find the collection by its existing title, or Content > Blog posts to find the post by its web page title.
- Rewrite within limits: title maximum 60 characters (the source reduced 73 to 54), meta description 140-160 with 160 as the hard maximum (167 reduced to 155-158). Count characters, not words - the source explicitly notes this.
- Save, then re-check: the audit tool will drop the warning on its next crawl once the length is compliant.
Caveat: The source notes default titles may omit brand terms because the team or agency is unaware - check the title actually reflects the store.
Caveat: Character limits are guidelines for display truncation, not ranking factors.

Tags: search, on-page-seo, meta-description, title-tags, shopify, site-audit
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

---

## Write and Place Alt Text for Store Images
Use when: Clearing missing/empty alt text warnings on product images, blog imagery and shared footer icons.
- Open the flagged page and inspect the missing image to see what it actually shows before writing anything (the source opens the image file directly).
- Search the page source for the image tag to confirm whether alt is empty or missing, which tells you whether you can fix it from the page or need a developer.
- Write the alt text as the image's subject in 3-4 words maximum; the source's examples are 'fast free shipping' for a shipping badge and 'brand trust 81%' for a data graphic, and rewriting a consumer-needs image around brand trust.
- In WordPress-style editors, add images via the editor's image block and use Edit Alt Text (the source found that simply dragging an image into the editor did not expose the alt option).
- For images living in shared template areas such as footers and headers, send the alt text to a developer - it applies site-wide rather than to one page.
Caveat: Each CMS exposes alt text differently - the source explicitly warns the option location varies.
Caveat: Do not stuff long keyword phrases into alt text; the source caps it at three to four words.

Tags: search, on-page-seo, alt-text, technical-seo, product-page, content-strategy
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

## Use Semrush Keyword Gap to Find Competitor Terms You Are Missing
Use when: Planning content or category expansion and deciding which competitor-driven keywords are safe and relevant to pursue.
- In Semrush's domain overview, note that traffic estimates and authority scores differ from other tools (the source saw 9.8K vs Ahrefs' 4-5K, and authority 35 vs DR 44) - assume tool-relative numbers only.
- Open organic competitors, pick a rival and read the two figures: common keywords (e.g. 931) and the competitor's total keywords (e.g. 2,953). Subtract the common set - roughly 2,000 terms here - to approximate keywords you do not target.
- Drill into specific missing terms (the source's example: a red light therapy mask keyword the competitor ranks for and the client does not) and inspect what the competitor's page does.
- Apply the source's hard filter: only pursue keywords that describe services or products you genuinely offer - never target a keyword for something you do not sell.
- Compare the keyword distribution buckets (top 3, 4-10, 11-20) across tools to see whether your gap is depth or breadth.
Caveat: Keyword gap counts are tool estimates of ranking overlap, not guaranteed traffic.
Caveat: The source shows Semrush details can require an upgrade - free tiers limit depth if you are not a paying user.

Tags: search, semrush, keyword-research, competitive-analysis, content-strategy, product-selection
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

## Track Add-to-Cart and Button Click Events Without Back-End Code
Use when: Store analytics phase where you need button-level engagement data for PDPs, forms or checkout-adjacent CTAs.
- Identify the button to track (the source's example starts with a submit button, then extends to add-to-cart, checkout and transaction clicks) and confirm it is a real interactive element on the live page.
- Create a GTM trigger for clicks on that element, using the element variables enabled in configuration (the source shows enabling click elements before writing triggers).
- Create a tag that fires on that trigger - a GA4 event tag in modern setups - and give it a name you can trace back to the button.
- Publish the container and verify with GTM Preview that the event fires on click in a real session.
- Keep GTM as the maintenance layer: when a button changes, update the trigger rather than reopening the theme code.
Caveat: The source mentions this can also be done with a developer, so treat GTM as the lower-dependency option rather than the only route.
Caveat: GTM's click trigger and variable interfaces change; confirm current naming before building.

Tags: search, google-tag-manager, event-tracking, measurement, cart, conversion-optimization
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

## Set up Add-to-Cart event tracking with Google Tag Manager without touching site code
Use when: You want to measure how many shoppers click Add to Cart (or Checkout/Purchase buttons) on your product pages but don't want to edit the store's backend code.
- Install the Google Tag Manager container on the site, then in GTM go to Tags > New and open Preview mode. Verify the GA4 configuration tag (G-XXXX) is already firing on the page you are testing.
- Load your product page in the Preview connection. Click the Add to Cart button and watch the event log: a new click entry appears (e.g. click #23). Open that click entry and inspect variables — confirm 'Click Text' shows 'Add to Cart' (or the exact button label). This tells you the correct trigger identifier.
- Create a new tag: Tag Configuration > Google Analytics > Google Analytics: GA4 Event. Enter your Measurement ID (the G- code from GA4 Admin > Data Streams). Set the event name, e.g. 'add_to_cart'.
- Add a trigger: New > choose 'Click – All Elements' > Some Clicks > set Click Text equals the exact button text (paste 'add to cart' without quotes). Save the trigger and tag.
- Submit > Publish the tag. Re-open Preview on the product page and click the button again — confirm the add_to_cart tag fires. It will then appear in GA4 events and can be used in funnel/exploration reports.
- Repeat for Checkout and Purchase buttons, using their own click texts (or a different variable such as a CSS class if two buttons share the same text). Do this on a test/staging container first if possible.
Caveat: This demo tracks clicks by visible button text — if the button text changes (localization, A/B tests, dynamic labels) the trigger breaks; consider using a unique element ID or CSS selector instead.
Caveat: Duplicate or repeated tags (e.g. an old add-to-cart tag left active) cause confusing double counting — the presenter deleted the demo tag to avoid confusion.
Caveat: GTM and GA4 interfaces change frequently; verify the current menu names and event/trigger options before following.
Caveat: Only clicks are tracked, not whether the cart actually updated server-side — a button click is a proxy, not a confirmed cart add.

Tags: search, analytics, google-tag-manager, ga4, event-tracking, checkout, conversion-optimization, measurement
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

---

## Diagnose where shoppers drop off in the add-to-cart-to-purchase flow
Use when: You suspect shoppers abandon between cart and checkout or between checkout and payment, and want data on which stage loses the most people before deciding where to optimize.
- Instrument three click events — add_to_cart, checkout (or begin_checkout) and purchase — using GTM click triggers as described in the tracking card.
- Let data accumulate, then in GA4 open an Exploration or Funnel report and compare event counts across the three steps for a chosen date range.
- Read the flow as a drop-off profile. The source's illustrative example: 100 add-to-cart, 60 checkout, 10 purchase — i.e. 40% lost between cart and checkout and roughly 83% lost between checkout and purchase. These are illustration figures from the presenter, not benchmarks.
- Direct optimization at the largest gap: heavy cart-to-checkout loss suggests cart UI, shipping-cost surprises or forced account creation; heavy checkout-to-purchase loss suggests payment options, form friction or trust signals.
- Re-measure after each change to confirm the drop-off actually narrowed rather than just moving to another step.
Caveat: The 100/60/10 figures are illustrative examples the presenter gave for reading the flow, not targets or industry benchmarks.
Caveat: Click-based events count button clicks, not confirmed completions — some clicks may not represent true funnel progress.
Caveat: GA4 sampling, bot traffic and ad blockers can distort counts; validate against the platform's own checkout or order reports before acting on large gaps.

Tags: search, analytics, ga4, funnel-analysis, conversion-optimization, checkout, measurement, cart-abandonment
Source: SEO Full Course 2026 _ SEO Tutorial For Beginners _ Search Engine Optimization Course _ Simplilearn.docx

## Keyword Magic Tool: seed keyword to qualified opportunity list
Use when: Starting keyword research for a product category or content plan and you need a prioritized list of realistic ranking targets before briefing content or product pages.
- Open SEO toolkit > Keyword Magic Tool and enter one broad seed keyword tied to your business (e.g. 'olive oil').
- Read the output columns: Volume = monthly searches; KD = Keyword Difficulty percentage (higher = harder to rank); Intent = what the searcher wants.
- Classify intent with the four types: Informational (wants to learn), Navigational (seeking a specific site/brand), Commercial (researching before purchase), Transactional (ready to buy/act).
- Filter KD with a custom range of 0–49 to strip out the hardest terms.
- Add a volume filter (e.g. minimum 5,000 searches/month) so remaining terms have real demand.
- Use the topic cluster column on the left to drill into categories and surface more opportunities.
- Click the icon next to any keyword to open the mini Keyword Overview: global and per-country volume, KD, SERP features, CPC, trend, related questions and SERP analysis.
- Check the SERP preview for an AI Overview appearing above organic results — for those terms you are also competing for the AI answer, not just a ranking.
- Add chosen keywords to a named list (e.g. 'Month 1 Focus') via the plus button; aim for 10–15 opportunities per list.
Caveat: KD is Semrush's own difficulty metric, not an absolute prediction of ranking success.
Caveat: Volume and KD figures are estimates that shift over time.
Caveat: The source advises starting with low-difficulty, decent-volume 'low hanging fruit' rather than the biggest keywords.

Tags: search, keyword-research, content-strategy, semrush, product-selection
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## AI Prompt Research: what customers ask ChatGPT and AI search
Use when: Deciding whether your brand appears in AI answers for buying-intent questions, and which prompts to target with content or PDP copy.
- Open the AI Visibility toolkit > Prompt Research and enter a high-level topic (e.g. 'olive oil'). Click Analyse.
- Read the summary bar: total monthly AI search volume, number of subtopics, prompts, intent types, brands and source domains.
- Drill into subtopics (e.g. 'olive oil and its benefits') to see individual prompts, the AI response, brands mentioned, and the sources cited.
- Click 'View Full Response' on a prompt to read the actual AI-generated answer — top paragraph, brands named, and buying tips.
- Scan whether your brand is mentioned and how prominently versus competitors for commercially relevant prompts like 'best olive oils to buy'.
- Decide whether the prompt is worth targeting: if your brand is absent or buried, it is a content or page-copy opportunity.
- For prompts you want to own, click Create Content to route into the Content toolkit (an add-on) to generate optimized articles or SEO briefs.
Caveat: Prompts are what users ask AI; keywords are what they type into Google — the source treats the intent as the same but the platforms differ.
Caveat: Content toolkit (AI Article Generator, SEO Brief Generator) is a paid add-on beyond Semrush One with a separate seven-day trial.
Caveat: AI answers and cited sources change frequently, so re-check prompts periodically.

Tags: search, aeo, ai-visibility, customer-research, content-strategy, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Competitor Organic Rankings: read their organic playbook
Use when: Merchandising or content planning when you want to understand which topics and intents a direct competitor is winning and where they are exposed.
- Open SEO toolkit > Organic Rankings and enter a competitor domain (e.g. graza.co).
- Review their top keywords and which terms they perform best on in organic search.
- Check which SERP features they are capturing to see the format of results they win.
- Look at the balance of keywords by intent over time — the source example shows a competitor getting most traffic from informational keywords.
- Track how their positions and SERP features have changed over time to spot rising or declining topics.
- Treat any topic a competitor ranks highly for as a candidate that AI tools may also pull from, so it doubles as an AEO target list.
Caveat: The source notes the competitor example was chosen as a real brand with no affiliation.
Caveat: Rankings and SERP features are snapshots and vary by location and device.

## Keyword Gap: find keywords competitors rank for and you don't
Use when: Building a content or category-page roadmap where you want to close specific ranking gaps against named competitors.
- Open SEO toolkit > Keyword Gap, enter your domain in the first field.
- Use Add Domain to pick Semrush-suggested competitors from the dropdown (add up to three more) or type domains manually. Click Compare.
- Open the Missing tab to see keywords competitors rank for where your column shows zero — these are uncontested opportunities.
- Open the Weak tab to see keywords you do rank for but far below competitors.
- Prioritize Weak-tab keywords that combine a low KD with meaningful volume. In the example, a term where competitors rank #1 and #2 but your site ranks #36 has KD 41 and 1,000+ searches/month — flagged as a strong target.
- Add chosen keywords to your keyword list via the plus button.
- Select a handful of important terms to actively compete on and push into your content or category plan.
Caveat: Position numbers show competitor SERP rank, not their traffic.
Caveat: Zero in your column can mean no ranking at all, not a low position.
Caveat: Difficulty and volume are estimates; validate before committing significant production effort.

Tags: search, keyword-research, competitor-research, content-strategy, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Cluster your keyword list into a content calendar
Use when: Turning a scattered keyword list into a concrete monthly content or category-page plan with defined primary intent per page.
- Open Keyword Strategy Builder and select your existing list (e.g. 'Month 1 Focus').
- Review the quick stats: total keywords, combined monthly search volume, and average KD. The example list has 21 keywords, 136,230 monthly volume and 20.05% average KD.
- Sort or filter the list by volume, keyword, difficulty percentage or CPC to organize priorities.
- Click 'Cluster This List'. If prompted that the list has too few keywords to create profitable pages, either add more keywords or click 'Cluster List Anyway' — the source notes results improve with more keywords.
- After processing (may take minutes), review the clusters: each cluster = one page. Expanding a cluster shows the recommended keywords to target and its primary intent (e.g. commercial).
- Note the cluster's average KD and combined potential search volume (example: ~22% KD and 55,900 searches/month).
- Send the cluster keywords to Position Tracking to monitor results, to Create Content/Brief for production, or Export as a spreadsheet.
- Pick your first five clusters as the content calendar for the next month.
Caveat: Clustering on a short list produces weaker results, per the tool's own warning and the source's agreement.
Caveat: Average KD is a cluster-level average and does not guarantee any individual keyword will rank.
Caveat: Content toolkit destinations require an add-on subscription.

Tags: search, keyword-research, content-strategy, experiment-design, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Topic Research: headlines and questions that already work
Use when: Planning blog or category-page content when you need headline angles and questions that already resonate before briefing a writer.
- Open SEO toolkit > Topic Research, enter a high-level topic (e.g. 'olive oil') and click Get Content Ideas.
- Review subtopic ideas (example outputs: Mediterranean Diet, Type 2 Diabetes, Heart Disease, Anti-Inflammatory).
- Get more specific by searching longer keywords from your keyword strategy (e.g. 'best olive oil') — outputs become more targeted (Grocery Stores, Cold Pressed, Organic Extra Virgin Olive Oil).
- Click into a subtopic to see headline ideas ranked best-to-worst with resonance signals — the source example shows an article with 552 backlinks.
- Open the referenced article to study exactly what made it resonate.
- Favorite useful headlines and the audience questions surfaced (e.g. 'What is the best olive oil to buy in the USA?').
- Collect everything in the Favorite Ideas tab as your content planning board; aim for 10–15 favorited ideas across a few topics.
- Use the pattern: mix and match the best parts of the top-performing headlines into your own, then answer the favorited questions inside the article.
Caveat: Backlink counts on example articles are illustrative resonance signals, not benchmarks you should expect.
Caveat: Favorited ideas are a queue to work from, not a guarantee of performance.

Tags: search, content-strategy, customer-research, creative-strategy, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## SEO Writing Assistant: score and fix a draft before publishing
Use when: Reviewing a finished or near-finished product description, blog post or landing page copy before it goes live.
- Open SEO Writing Assistant and click Analyse My Text.
- Import text from an existing URL or paste your draft directly.
- Paste your target keywords from the Keyword Strategy Builder cluster into the field provided so the tool scores against them.
- Click Get Recommendations and read the overall score (10-point scale) plus the axis chart covering tone of voice, readability, SEO and originality.
- Work through the issue list on the left; click any issue to see the proposed fix in the floating bar and accept, reject or retry. Example fixes: a title over 60 characters that may be truncated in search results, and hard-to-read sentences the tool offers to simplify.
- Iterate — the source notes new issues can emerge after fixes, and perfect scores are unrealistic. Aim to move the score up (their example improved by 0.3) and reduce the issue count.
- Correcting the title also improved readability in the example by removing filler words like 'actually'.
Caveat: Readability, tone and SEO scores are heuristics, not ranking guarantees.
Caveat: The tool does not assess commercial persuasiveness — pair it with your own judgment.
Caveat: Fixing toward the tool's suggestions can flatten voice if applied uncritically.

Tags: search, content-strategy, conversion-optimization, product-page, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Site Audit: fix errors first, warnings second
Use when: Before a content or merchandising push, when you need to confirm product and category pages are crawlable and not returning errors.
- Open SEO toolkit > Site Audit, select your project (created from the domain entered on the welcome screen).
- Read the Site Health indicator — the source example shows 87% versus a 92% top-10% benchmark, a 5% gap.
- Check the AI search health score separately (example: 75% with 80% flagged as the competing threshold).
- Open the Errors count first — these are the more severe issues. Click an issue type grouped by number of pages (e.g. 'Two pages returned 4XX status code').
- Verify broken links by opening the page via the icon; a 404 confirms it goes nowhere real.
- Use the 'How to Fix' link for each issue — it explains the cause and remediation steps. Use Share to hand the issue to whoever owns the fix.
- Fix red critical errors, then orange warnings, then blue notices. The source says not to panic at a long issue list and not to aim for perfection — just reduce the count.
- Treat audits as recurring, not one-off — the source frames this as the foundation step before tracking and content work.
Caveat: Health and AI-search-health percentages and the 92%/80% comparison points are Semrush-presented benchmarks in this example, not universal targets.
Caveat: The AI-health panel may report pages 'Blocked from AI search' and point you to robots.txt; see the llms.txt card for that specific fix.
Caveat: Google Search does not use llms.txt or require special AI text files. Treat other-engine support as unverified unless documented by that engine.

Tags: search, technical-seo, site-audit, measurement, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

---

## robots.txt and llms.txt: crawlability for Google and for AI
Use when: When Site Audit reports pages blocked from AI search, or when launching or restructuring a store and want AI tools to surface your key pages.
- In Site Audit, check the AI Search Health panel for 'Blocked from AI search' — the source example shows 100 pages crawled and 4 blocked per AI tool.
- Click 'How To Unblock Pages' to see the recommended action, typically reviewing the robots.txt file.
- Understand the two files: robots.txt tells Google which pages to crawl; llms.txt does the same job for AI tools.
- Create llms.txt as a plain text file at yourdomain.com/llms.txt describing which pages on your site are most important and what they are about, so ChatGPT, Perplexity and others can prioritize them.
- If the site lacks an llms.txt file, the source says it is worth adding. Use the 'How to Fix' link for issue-specific guidance and resources.
- Re-run the audit after adding the files to confirm blocked-page counts drop.
Caveat: llms.txt is an emerging convention — AI tools' support for it may vary and change over time.
Caveat: Verify current file-format and crawler requirements, as AI bot behavior is evolving.
Caveat: Google Search does not use llms.txt or require special AI text files. Treat other-engine support as unverified unless documented by that engine.

Tags: search, aeo, technical-seo, site-audit, crawlability, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Backlinks overview: authority, best links and lost links
Use when: When planning link building or diagnosing a drop in domain authority and organic visibility for a store.
- Open SEO toolkit > Link Building > Backlinks, enter your domain and click Analyse.
- Read the top bar: number of referring sites, total links, and monthly visits driven by those links (example: 2,600 referring sites, 28,000 links, 161,000 monthly visits).
- Check the Authority Score — Semrush's metric combining number and quality of backlinks plus overall site authenticity. The source example shows 47, described as 'pretty good', with higher being better.
- View the authority score trend over time on the adjacent panel.
- Open the Backlinks tab and filter by 'Best' to see links that raise your authority because they come from reputable sites.
- Filter by 'Lost and Vital' to find links that used to point to your site and no longer do.
- For a high-value, high-page-authority lost link, reach out to the site — it may be broken and need updating, or the site may have deliberately dropped you. Either way, investigate to try to restore it.
- Treat backlinks as valuable for both Google trust and AI citation, since the source notes AI tools tend to cite sources other sites also cite.
Caveat: You do not need to act on every link in the list — prioritize high-value, high-authority lost links only.
Caveat: Authority Score is a Semrush metric, not Google's; use it for relative comparison.
Caveat: The source example does not establish a target authority number.

Tags: search, link-building, measurement, authority, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Backlink Gap: find sites linking to competitors but not you
Use when: Planning outreach to earn links and AI-cited authority in your niche, especially when competitor roundups or listicles already exist.
- Open SEO toolkit > Competitive Analysis > Backlink Gap.
- Confirm your domain sits in the first field labelled 'you', add several competitors, then click Find Prospects.
- Read the columns: referring domain, its authority score, the Matches count (how many of the input domains it links to), and links per destination domain. A zero means that domain does not link to that destination.
- Filter by Authority Score with a lower threshold (the source uses 50) and Apply.
- Scan for patterns — if one site links to several competitors but not you, it is a strong outreach candidate. In the source example, the Food Network links to three competitors but not the target brand.
- Weigh relevance and reach: the same example site has a high authority score and 22.5 million monthly visits.
- Treat the filtered list as a checklist. Tick the box next to a referring domain and click Start Outreach to open the Backlink Builder tool for managing the campaign.
Caveat: The authority threshold of 50 is the source's suggested starting filter, not a universal rule.
Caveat: The source states a few high-quality backlinks from relevant niche sites beat hundreds from random directories, but does not quantify the difference.
Caveat: Outreach outcomes depend on editorial fit; a competitor mention does not guarantee coverage.

Tags: search, link-building, competitor-research, acquisition, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Position Tracking: monitor Google rankings and AI summaries together
Use when: After publishing new content or making technical fixes, to verify movement and catch visibility changes early.
- Open SEO toolkit > Position Tracking and select your SEO project to land on the Landscape tab.
- Read the headline metrics: visibility score (example 65.72% with a potential 66.02 over three months), estimated traffic, and average position per keyword.
- Review top keywords and open the Rankings Overview for the full keyword list.
- Check whether keywords appear in the AI summary — the example shows 'Agave versus honey' ranking first in the AI summary.
- Click the magnifier/browser icon on any keyword to view a cached snapshot of the live search result page.
- Expand any keyword to see its position change over time; more history accumulates the longer your account runs.
- Import keywords from Keyword Strategy Builder: open your list, tick the checkbox, click Send Keywords > Position Tracking > Apply, select the project and confirm.
- Accept duplicate warnings (the example sends 19 keywords and skips 2 duplicates) and click Go To Position Tracking.
- Filter by a specific keyword in the Overview tab to isolate its trend.
- Track your 20–30 most important keywords and check back weekly to see movement in both Google and AI search.
Caveat: The visibility score and its projected improvement are Semrush metrics, not traffic guarantees.
Caveat: You need accumulated history before position-change charts become informative.
Caveat: Cached SERP snapshots may differ from what a live user sees by location.

Tags: search, rank-tracking, measurement, aeo, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

- Open SEO toolkit > Domain Overview, enter your domain and click Search.
- Read the high-level standings for both AI search visibility and traditional SEO visibility side by side.
- Scroll to the traffic-by-type-over-time chart and toggle between AI search and Google search.
- Review distribution by country and cited sources, including branded versus non-branded splits.
- Act on early signals — the source example shows a brand with declining traffic and frames catching such a trend early as the goal.

## AI Visibility Overview: which LLMs mention and cite you
Use when: Deciding which AI platform to prioritize for AEO work, or reporting brand presence in AI answers to stakeholders.
- Open the AI Visibility toolkit > Visibility Overview.
- Set the region to your primary market (the source example switches from worldwide to the US for a US-focused brand) to get relevant numbers.
- Read the AI visibility score out of 100 — the example brand scores 50, described as occasionally mentioned but improvable.
- Review monthly mentions (example: ~2,300) and the trend of mentions vs citations vs cited pages over time.
- Check mentions by country to see where the brand is actually surfaced — the example shows the US dominating mentions.
- Check distribution by LLM: in the example, ChatGPT rarely mentions the brand while Google AI Overview does most of the heavy lifting.
- Toggle to see which LLM cites the brand's pages most. Focus effort on the LLMs underperforming — the source suggests prioritizing ChatGPT and Gemini in that example.
- Use the 'What's Next' section: 'Uncover Topic Opportunities' lists topics to cover to earn more LLM mentions, and 'Your Performing Topics' shows the topics already driving mentions, with the prompts behind them.
Caveat: AI visibility scores are Semrush's composite metrics and the 50/100 example is illustrative, not a benchmark.
Caveat: LLM behavior and coverage change rapidly, so re-check the region and platform splits periodically.
Caveat: Worldwide versus single-region settings materially change the numbers.

## Brand Performance: how ChatGPT talks about you and what to fix
Use when: Setting AEO and brand-messaging priorities when you need to know how AI assistants position you versus competitors for buying-intent questions.
- Open the AI Visibility toolkit > Brand Performance, enter your domain, click Analyse.
- Select the target location (e.g. United States) and click Get Started, then allow time for the report to load.
- Read the Insights panel for how ChatGPT discusses the brand across conversations — e.g. strong sentiment at 78% but the lowest share of voice, with the recommendation to push high-visibility campaigns to grow volume without losing positivity.
- Review overall sentiment in LLM responses and the key business drivers section. Example: the brand ranks second on design-led appeal but lags on freshness, with a suggestion to amplify harvest, origin and testing signals.
- Scroll to Strategy Recommendations and read them across timeframes.
- Act on the urgent-timeline recommendations first this week, then short, then medium timeframe items.
- Use the recommendations as concrete positioning inputs — in the example they include codifying a simple promise (e.g. 'chef-worthy California EVOO for everyday cooking'), and addressing that the brand is seen as a specialty upgrade rather than a default everyday choice.
Caveat: The sentiment percentages, rankings and recommendations are generated for the source's example brand and are not transferable benchmarks.
Caveat: The report can take a while to generate and reflects a point in time; recommendations should be re-read as data refreshes.
Caveat: Strategy recommendations are AI-generated suggestions, not guaranteed outcomes — pair them with your own category knowledge.

Tags: search, aeo, ai-visibility, positioning, measurement, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## The four-step search loop: research, organize, fix, track
Use when: Setting up or resetting an ongoing SEO/AEO program for a store and you need an ordered operating sequence rather than ad hoc tasks.
- Step 1 — Research: find what people search on Google (Keyword Magic Tool) and what they ask AI (Prompt Research). The source treats keywords and prompts as the same underlying intent on different platforms.
- Step 2 — Organize: turn research into topic clusters via Keyword Strategy Builder, then decide what content or pages to create. Pick your first five clusters as the month's calendar.
- Step 3 — Fix the foundation: run a Site Audit for errors and AI crawlability (robots.txt, llms.txt), and close backlink gaps with Backlink Gap.
- Step 4 — Track: monitor Google rankings and AI visibility side by side in Position Tracking and Domain Overview, then repeat the loop.
- Treat this as cyclical, not one-time — the source states SEO and AEO are make-changes-then-watch-what-happens activities, and without tracking you are guessing.
Caveat: The source's recommended Semrush One starter plan is $199/month; the source notes it is not cheap.
Caveat: Several destinations in the workflow (Content toolkit, AI Article Generator, SEO Brief Generator) require add-on subscriptions with separate trials.
Caveat: Promotional trial and pricing details change — verify current plans and interfaces before purchasing.
Caveat: Google Search does not use llms.txt or require special AI text files. Treat other-engine support as unverified unless documented by that engine.

Tags: search, content-strategy, aeo, measurement, experiment-design, semrush
Source: Semrush Tutorial 2026 - Master SEO & AEO with Semrush One.docx

## Keyword Research Workflow with Google Keyword Planner and Trends
Use when: Planning keyword targets before editing a homepage, product page, collection, or blog post's SEO fields.
- Open Google Ads and go to Tools > Planning > Keyword Planner (account required to see data). Set language and country to match your target market.
- Enter a seed term describing your product category (example: 'homemade soap'); read monthly search volume, competition level, and CPC range to gauge commercial value.
- Pick one main keyword and two supporting keywords per page. The source's demo chose 'homemade soap' as main (1,000–10,000 monthly searches, high competition) with lower-volume, more specific terms as support.
- Use Google Trends related searches (optionally with the Glimpse Chrome extension) to surface adjacent queries, then paste them back into Keyword Planner to check volume.
- Test a query in Google search and harvest autocomplete predictions as candidate keywords, then validate them in Keyword Planner.
- Optionally paste a competitor's URL into Keyword Planner's 'start with website' option to see which keywords their pages already rank for, and choose gaps to target.
- Assign each page a distinct keyword set based on that page's content; don't reuse the same set across all products.
Caveat: Volume/CPC shown are estimates from Google's tools, not guarantees.
Caveat: Higher-end tools (Ahrefs, Semrush) give richer intent and country data but cost more; Keyword Planner is the free fallback.

Tags: search, keyword-research, product-selection, product-page, collection-page, google-keyword-planner, seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Separate Informational vs Transactional Search Intent
Use when: Deciding whether a keyword belongs on a product/collection page, a blog post, or nowhere in your catalog.
- Classify each keyword: informational (e.g., 'how to make homemade soap') vs transactional (e.g., 'homemade soap for sale', 'homemade soap near me').
- Route transactional keywords to product and collection pages, where the visitor can immediately buy.
- Route informational keywords to blog posts and guide pages that link internally to the relevant products.
- When using tools with explicit intent labels (e.g., Semrush), use them to confirm intent instead of guessing.
- Favor keywords with buying intent when the page's goal is revenue; use informational terms to build topical authority and feed internal links to money pages.
Caveat: Intent labels are tool heuristics and can be wrong; spot-check the live SERP if in doubt.
Caveat: Informational traffic converts far less directly — judge it on assisted discovery, not last-click sales.

Tags: search, keyword-research, search-intent, content-strategy, product-page, collection-page, seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Edit Global Store SEO: Homepage Title and Meta Description
Use when: Setting or fixing the homepage listing that appears in Google results for brand and category searches.
- In Shopify admin, go to Online Store > Preferences and find the Search engine listing section.
- Write a title that includes the main keyword; respect the 70-character field limit. The source's demo used 63 characters with the main keyword plus supporting terms.
- Write a meta description containing supporting keywords and optionally a call to action; keep it accurate to the brand voice.
- Optionally draft candidates with ChatGPT, then edit so they genuinely represent your brand — avoid overly broad keywords that don't match your niche.
- Save, then verify by searching the keyword in Google to see how your listing renders.
Caveat: The title/description you write here is the homepage listing only; each product, collection, page, and blog post has its own listing fields.
Caveat: Field character limits may change in Shopify's interface — verify current limits.

Tags: search, homepage, seo, title-tag, meta-description, shopify-admin
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Enforce a Single H1 Tag Per Page
Use when: Auditing a theme or custom section for heading structure on product, collection, homepage, or custom-built pages.
- Inspect the page in the browser and check which element carries the H1. On Shopify's Dawn theme, the homepage H1 is typically the logo; on collection pages the collection title is the H1; on product pages the product title is the H1.
- Check custom sections a developer or you built — they sometimes introduce an extra H1.
- If two H1s exist, demote one to H2 or lower so only the intended main title remains H1.
- After fixing, re-inspect to confirm only one H1 remains on every page type.
Caveat: Theme behavior differs; the logo-as-H1 behavior is stated for Dawn and may not hold for all themes — verify in your theme.

Tags: search, on-page-seo, heading-structure, theme-audit, seo, dawn-theme
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Write SEO Product Titles from Specs Using Keyword Data
Use when: Rewriting vague product names (e.g., 'Sweet Honey Bomb') that don't tell Google or shoppers what the product is.
- Copy the product's specs/description into ChatGPT and ask for candidate high-volume keywords relevant to those specs.
- Validate those keywords in Google Keyword Planner; discard ones with weak volume or poor relevance.
- Feed the validated keyword list back into ChatGPT asking for title options; pick the best and include at least the main keyword plus a supporting keyword.
- Paste the title into the Shopify product Title field and save.
- For the description, ask ChatGPT to write from the specs, instructing it to weave in the researched keywords but keep the text natural (not obviously AI-generated). Aim for at least 100 words; the source says 300–400 words is ideal for product copy.
- Format the description — bold key points, add images inside it, and adjust theme title font size in the customizer if the longer title breaks layout.
- Edit the result so it matches your brand voice before publishing.
Caveat: AI output must be reviewed for accuracy and brand fit; don't publish unedited.
Caveat: Longer titles may require theme typography adjustments to avoid layout breakage.

Tags: search, product-page, product-copy, title-tag, keyword-research, chatgpt, seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Image SEO: Alt Text, File Names, and Formatting
Use when: Preparing product photos, banners, and gallery media before or after uploading to Shopify.
- Write alt text that combines the target keyword, the product name, and a visual descriptor: the source's example is 'summer fragment bath bomb purple frontal' (keyword + product + color + angle).
- Reuse the alt text to rename the actual image file so the file name is descriptive rather than a string of numbers/gibberish.
- Work through every image in the product's media gallery, then go to Content > Files and fix the alt text and file names for the store's media library.
- Optionally verify by inspecting the image on the storefront to confirm the descriptive URL is live.
Caveat: Alt text also serves accessibility for visually impaired shoppers — describe the image honestly, don't just stuff keywords.
Caveat: Compress images before upload (keep files under 2MB) to avoid hurting page speed.

Tags: search, image-seo, alt-text, product-page, accessibility, page-speed, seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Write Product Meta Descriptions That Earn Clicks
Use when: Filling the per-product Search engine listing meta description field in Shopify.
- Take the product description and ask ChatGPT to produce a ~160-character search engine listing meta description.
- Review and edit for accuracy, brand voice, and a compelling reason to click; the source says the meta description is less about keywords and more about enticing the click.
- Paste it into the product's Search engine listing meta description field and save.
- If you have a large catalog and manual editing isn't feasible, use an SEO app to bulk-generate, and hand-edit top sellers.
- Use the listing preview to confirm how the title and description will render in Google results.
Caveat: Very large catalogs may not be practical to edit manually — prioritize high-value pages or use bulk tooling.
Caveat: Excerpts can also be left auto-derived if they already read well.

## Clean URL Handles and Enable 301 Redirects
Use when: Editing a product or collection URL handle in the Search engine listing preview.
- In the product/collection Search engine listing section, edit the URL handle to remove filler words and reflect the product/collection name (e.g., use the title with grammar and special characters stripped out).
- Before saving, confirm the checkbox that automatically creates a redirect from the old URL to the new one is checked.
- Save. Bookmarked old URLs then forward to the new page instead of returning a 404.
- Repeat for collections and any page whose handle you change; the handle should contain the keywords from the title so Google understands the page.
Caveat: If the redirect box is unchecked, existing bookmarks and external links break with 404 errors and you lose those visits.
Caveat: Redirect handling in Shopify's UI may vary by version — verify the checkbox location.

Tags: search, url-management, 301-redirects, product-page, collection-page, technical-seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

---

## Optimize Collection Pages Without Pushing Products Down
Use when: Improving a collection page (e.g., 'Bath') that currently has no descriptive content.
- Do keyword research for the collection and write a descriptive title that names the products it contains (the source's example reframes a vague 'Bath' into a title describing the scent/type of products).
- Write a collection description of roughly 100–400 words containing the right keywords so Google can crawl and understand the page.
- In the Shopify theme editor, move the collection description below the product grid if you want products visible first — keep the text on the page regardless.
- Set the collection's Search engine listing title and meta description, and update the URL handle to remove filler words.
- Confirm the automatic redirect checkbox is checked so the old collection URL forwards to the new one.
- Repeat for every collection on the store.
Caveat: The source notes that many merchants skip collection descriptions because they push products down; moving the block resolves the tension.

Tags: search, collection-page, on-page-seo, merchandising, meta-description, seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Optimize Pages and Blog Posts, Then Interlink Them
Use when: Extending SEO beyond products and collections, and setting up internal linking between blog content and products.
- For every Shopify page and blog post, fill in the title (containing the main keyword plus optionally a secondary), the content/description (with keywords and real value), and the Search engine listing meta description and handle.
- Publish new content regularly: the source recommends at least two posts a month, ideally one per week, researched through Keyword Planner and relevant to what you sell.
- Add internal links wherever there is a natural opportunity — for example, link from a blog post or benefits page directly to the relevant product in the body content.
- Link broadly across pages, posts, and products so that crawling one page reveals the next, helping Google discover more pages faster.
Caveat: Posting frequency is the source's recommendation, not a documented ranking threshold — treat it as a practical cadence.

Tags: search, blog, internal-linking, on-page-seo, content-strategy, seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Mobile-Friendliness and Page Speed Checks for Ranking
Use when: Auditing a theme before or after launch for mobile and speed problems that suppress rankings.
- In Chrome, right-click > Inspect, toggle device view, and preview as a device (e.g., iPhone 12 Pro); check that nothing overflows the screen, images aren't distorted, and all content is reachable. In Safari, use Develop > Enter Responsive Design mode.
- Run Google PageSpeed Insights on your store URL and read the field data — the source notes these graphs reflect the past 28 days, so fixes won't show there immediately.
- Compress all images before upload (keep under 2MB; a page should total roughly 2MB, and an uncompressed 1.4MB image is a problem). The source recommends a free compression service; an SEO app can also bulk-optimize.
- Do NOT lazy-load above-the-fold images (e.g., a product hero or homepage banner) — these are typically the Largest Contentful Paint element and lazy-loading them worsens LCP. Other off-screen images can use loading=lazy.
- Audit installed apps: third-party app code is out of your control and can slow the store; uninstall anything unused (you can reinstall later) and keep only apps that bring real value.
- Re-run the speed test and fix the flagged issues you can act on.
Caveat: Most modern Shopify themes are mobile-friendly out of the box, so mobile checks often confirm rather than fix.
Caveat: PageSpeed field data lags ~28 days — don't expect instant improvement in the graphs.

Tags: search, page-speed, mobile-friendly, lcp, lazy-loading, image-optimization, technical-seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Add Rich Snippets (FAQ and Product Schema)
Use when: Wanting extra visible SERP real estate for product, FAQ, or article content.
- Add a lightweight, accessible FAQ answering genuine pre-purchase questions; do not justify it with promised FAQ rich results.
- Add the schema either via custom code (the source references a guide) or via an SEO app that generates FAQ and review schema.
- For app-generated snippets, toggle the desired type on (e.g., FAQ, product grid, image) and run the app's built-in test to confirm the snippet is correctly injected.
- Monitor Search Console for rich result eligibility and errors.
Caveat: Structured data markup requirements and Google's supported rich result types change over time — verify current specs before investing in custom code.
Caveat: Rich snippets make listings larger and more clickable but don't guarantee ranking.
Caveat: Use store FAQs to answer shopper questions. Do not promise Google FAQ rich results or treat FAQ markup as a ranking requirement; verify current search feature support.

Tags: search, rich-snippets, schema-markup, faq, product-page, technical-seo
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Submit the Shopify XML Sitemap to Search Console
Use when: Finalizing technical SEO setup after optimizing pages so Google can crawl and index them.
- First confirm the correct domain is primary: Settings > Domains, set your custom (non-myshopify) domain as primary. The source stresses doing this BEFORE submitting the sitemap so Google crawls the right URLs.
- View your sitemap at yourstore.com/sitemap.xml and copy the URL.
- In Google Search Console, create/link the property for your site, then go to Indexing > Sitemaps and submit the sitemap URL.
- Google will begin crawling and indexing the site; if you changed the primary domain afterward, submit a new sitemap.
Caveat: Sitemap path and Search Console navigation can change — verify current interface.
Caveat: Submitting a sitemap requests crawling; it does not guarantee indexing or ranking.
Caveat: Sitemaps support discovery; submission does not guarantee crawling, indexing or rankings.

Tags: search, sitemap, google-search-console, technical-seo, domain-setup
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

Use when: Setting up or refreshing store branding that feeds external sales channels and search appearances.
- Locate the brand settings tab (the source used Shopify Sidekick to find the link); add logos, colors, and brand identity assets.
- Ensure the brand data is consistent, since other sales channels like Facebook and Google pull this information to represent you online.
- Revisit when adding new channels or products so listings stay consistent.
Caveat: The exact location of brand settings may shift in Shopify admin — verify in your account.

## Build Backlinks and Recurring Content for Authority
Use when: Planning off-page SEO and content cadence to lift overall ranking potential.
- Pursue backlinks from authoritative sites: partner with people who run large websites, or work with influencers who can mention and link to your store in their articles.
- Treat authority as transferable — being featured on a high-authority site passes some of that trust to your store.
- Publish new content regularly; the source recommends at least 2 posts/month and ideally weekly.
- Research each planned post with Keyword Planner and write on topics close to what you sell and that your customers want to learn about.
- For each post, set the title with the main (and optionally secondary) keyword, put the keywords in the content, add a click-worthy meta description, and include a keyword-bearing URL handle.
- Use an SEO app to track existing backlinks and find broken links to fix.
Caveat: The posting-frequency suggestion is the presenter's opinion, not a documented Google requirement.
Caveat: Backlink effects are slow and depend on the linking site's authority.

Tags: search, backlinks, off-page-seo, content-strategy, authority, blog
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Audit Existing Rankings to Fix Wasted Content
Use when: Doing a periodic SEO review to redirect content toward terms that actually drive traffic.
- Use an SEO app's 'analyze my keywords' feature (the source shows SEOAnt) to list keywords you currently rank for.
- Flag pages ranking for keywords with no search volume or no commercial relevance (the source notes blog posts often do this).
- Adjust that content or page to target more useful keywords instead.
- Optionally monitor competitor sites by entering their URL to track the keywords they rank for and how they change over time, then adapt strategies that fit your budget.
Caveat: Competitor-tracking and keyword-analysis features may sit behind paid app tiers.
Caveat: Prioritize pages with business value; not every irrelevant ranking needs fixing.

Tags: search, keyword-research, content-audit, competitive-research, seo-apps, measurement
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Deciding Whether to Automate Shopify SEO with an App
Use when: Deciding how to execute SEO at scale across a large catalog.
- Assess scope: manual editing of every product/collection/page listing is impractical for large catalogs, so prioritize manual work on high-value pages.
- Use an SEO app for bulk tasks the source demonstrated: compressing all product/collection images, generating per-page SEO scores with actionable fix pointers, injecting rich snippets without code, and finding/fixing broken links.
- Treat app SEO scores as prioritization guides, not goals: the demo raised a product page from bad to 91 by adding a keyword-bearing H2, then to 94 by adding in-description images and a keyword in the meta description without hitting 100.
- Use the app's built-in keyword-research and rank-tracking tools as a partial replacement for Keyword Planner/Ahrefs.
- Try the free tier for a small store; upgrade only if the workload justifies it.
Caveat: The app recommendation is promotional in the source; the presenter discloses a discount code. Evaluate features and pricing yourself and verify the current interface.
Caveat: Score targets are app-specific heuristics, not Google ranking rules.
Caveat: Third-party apps add load and can affect page speed.

Tags: search, seo-apps, automation, product-page, image-optimization, rich-snippets, measurement
Source: Shopify SEO Optimization Guide for Beginners 2026.docx

## Deciding whether SEO is worth pursuing for a Shopify store
Use when: Deciding whether to invest budget, time or agency fees into SEO for your store, before writing any content.
- Ask the core question first: do people already search for what you sell? SEO only captures existing demand, it does not create it. The source (Dave Fogerty) argues that if you invented a genuinely new product nobody searches for, SEO cannot help.
- Check Google search volume and commercial intent for your main category terms. If people rarely Google your category, content will not rank or convert.
- Test margin math: SEO favours stores with healthy AOV and margins. If AOV is low or margins are razor thin, the content investment may never pay back.
- Recognise the source's binary framing: for most Shopify brands SEO is either the most profitable channel or a huge waste of time, with little middle ground.
- Treat this as a channel-diversification play, not a replacement: the source's Calming Blankets example kept selling via SEO when Meta ads broke repeatedly, but that is one brand's case, not a guarantee.
- If those tests pass, commit to bottom-funnel, conversion-intent keywords rather than general blog traffic, which the source says is close to worthless in 2025.
Caveat: Source's 10M first-year revenue and #1 ranking in 6 months are one brand's anecdote, not a benchmark.
Caveat: The 'SEO is all or nothing' framing is the presenter's opinion, not a measured rule.

Tags: search, seo, channel-strategy, organic-traffic, shopify, budget-allocation
Source: Steal These Shopify SEO Hacks To Rank #1 Overnight.docx

## Capturing branded search your paid ads create
Use when: Planning paid acquisition or auditing whether competitors outrank you on your own brand and category terms.
- Accept that Meta and other paid ads create latent demand: people see the ad, don't click, then Google your brand or product later. The source calls this branded search.
- Check whether your site ranks for your own brand name and the generic product term your ads promote.
- If you don't rank, a smaller competitor can appear first and take the sale you paid to generate. The source frames this as one brand funding awareness while another captures conversion.
- Protect the conversion path by ensuring a product or collection page (not just homepage) ranks for the branded and category search.
- Pair this with the source's keyword-structure hack: build collection pages targeting the variations people Google after seeing an ad.
- This is a defensive play on existing spend, not a reason to add SEO budget on top of everything else.
Caveat: Source gives no data on how much paid spend leaks to competitors; treat the mechanism as a hypothesis to verify in your own Search Console.
Caveat: Requires that people actually Google your category, which the source says is not universal.

Tags: search, seo, acquisition, branded-search, paid-ads, shopify
Source: Steal These Shopify SEO Hacks To Rank #1 Overnight.docx

---

## Multiplying collection pages to target keyword variations
Use when: Merchandising a product category where you currently have a single broad collection page competing on one high-difficulty term.
- Take your bestselling category and list the different ways people search for it: e.g., hoodies splits into men's hoodies, oversized hoodies, zip hoodies, running hoodies, hoodies under $50.
- Build a separate collection (or landing) page for each variation. You can include the same products; you're changing presentation, not inventory.
- Validate each keyword in Ahrefs or Semrush: set the keyword difficulty filter below 35 and look for commercial intent (check the current interface, these tools change).
- Sanity-check by Googling the keyword: if other collection pages rank, you likely can too. If only giants like Nike rank, it's probably too competitive.
- Respect one keyword per page and never target the same keyword with both a product page and a collection page. The source calls this keyword cannibalization and says it will tank results.
- This converts one unwinnable competitive term into many smaller, easier terms that accumulate traffic.
Caveat: Keyword difficulty guidance (<35) is a source-specific heuristic, not a universal threshold.
Caveat: Requires genuine search variation; splitting a category into terms nobody searches adds no value.

Tags: search, seo, keyword-research, collections, merchandising, shopify, ahrefs, semrush
Source: Steal These Shopify SEO Hacks To Rank #1 Overnight.docx

---

## The four blog formats that still drive ecommerce revenue
Use when: Planning a content calendar or auditing existing blog posts that get traffic but no sales.
- Stop producing the formats the source calls dead in ecommerce: seasonal lifestyle posts, generic listicles, trend pieces, and tips that apply to everyone and sell to no one.
- Format 1 - You vs competitors: target 'brand A vs brand B' searches by decision-stage buyers. Mentioning a competitor's name can help you rank for their branded search and introduce yourself as the stronger option.
- Format 2 - Alternatives to competitors: target 'alternatives to X' and 'best X alternatives' searches, list options and position yourself first to convert people outside your funnel.
- Format 3 - Best [product type] for [use case]: target clear buying intent like 'best eye serums for women over 50'; match product to need and target low-difficulty longtail terms.
- Format 4 - Buying guides: long-form evergreen content that educates hesitant buyers and naturally earns backlinks that lift domain authority.
- Before writing any post, check the SERP: if blogs rank, write the blog; if it's all collection pages, build that page instead.
- The source claims 10-15 well-targeted posts can build this system, and cites a client (Simbotanica) ranking #3 for a competitor's brand name within a week of implementing two competitor-focused formats.
Caveat: The Simbotanica ranking-in-a-week result is an anecdote, not a benchmark.
Caveat: Only works for products with existing search demand.

Tags: search, seo, content-strategy, comparison-pages, buying-guides, bottom-funnel, shopify
Source: Steal These Shopify SEO Hacks To Rank #1 Overnight.docx

---

## Building an internal linking system that funnels authority to money pages
Use when: Optimising an existing store with orphaned blog posts and product pages that never get internal links.
- Identify your money pages: the collection and product pages where conversion happens.
- Write top- and mid-funnel blogs with the linking flow decided upfront, not bolted on later.
- Link from each blog to a money page within the first 100-200 words, then add three to five internal links to related blogs later in the article.
- Use descriptive anchor text of two to four words that matches the product. Avoid 'click here' and avoid using your brand name as anchor text.
- Link collections from general to specific: e.g., from a collagen supplements collection down to a hair loss collagen for women sub-collection.
- Start with your top two to three highest-traffic blog posts and add a clean relevant link from each to your bestselling collection or product page.
- Repeat until the site is a connected web. The source claims each link takes seconds and can meaningfully shift monthly revenue.
Caveat: Internal links transfer value but cannot fix a site with no external authority or no existing traffic.
Caveat: The $100 vs $1,000 per month difference is a source claim, not a measured outcome.

Tags: search, seo, internal-linking, site-architecture, collections, shopify, organic-traffic
Source: Steal These Shopify SEO Hacks To Rank #1 Overnight.docx

---

## Getting cited in AI search results (GEO)
Use when: Adapting your PDPs, collections and content for AI Overviews, Perplexity or ChatGPT-style answers that bypass the ten blue links.
- Accept the shift the source calls GEO: AI tools summarise multiple sources into one answer, so if you're not cited you may as well not exist for that query.
- Note the source's cited study of 768,000+ AI citations: over half came from product pages, comparison content and buying guides, not news or homepages.
- Split broad collections into specific use-case pages (e.g., mineral sunscreen for sensitive skin, mineral sunscreen for dry skin) reusing the same products but matching exact query language.
- Refresh comparison blogs ('brand A vs brand B', 'best collagen supplements for women over 40') so AI tools have citable, question-shaped content.
- Test by Googling your product type phrased as a question, e.g., 'What hoodie is best for me?', and study which pages get cited in any AI overview.
- Audit each collection: can it be split into individual needs or situations? The source says need-specific pages tend to outperform in both AI and organic search.
- Make cited pages skimable, useful and product-focused so both AI tools and humans can extract an answer.
Caveat: The 768,000-citation study is reported second-hand; treat the exact split as indicative, not verified.
Caveat: GEO is an emerging channel with uncertain durability; the source frames it as early-mover arbitrage.

Tags: search, seo, geo, ai-search, product-page, collections, content-strategy, shopify
Source: Steal These Shopify SEO Hacks To Rank #1 Overnight.docx
